     1→#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2→set -euo pipefail
     3→
     4→# Integration test for ReleaseBeacon.
     5→# Requires: docker, go, curl, jq
     6→#
     7→# Tests:
     8→#   1. Health endpoint
     9→#   2. Project CRUD via API
    10→#   3. Source creation via API
    11→#   4. GitHub webhook → release ingestion → River job
    12→#   5. Duplicate webhook idempotency
    13→#   6. Invalid signature rejection
    14→#   7. Channel & subscription CRUD
    15→
    16→SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
    17→PROJECT_ROOT="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/.." && pwd)"
    18→
    19→# --- Config ---
    20→CONTAINER_NAME="releaseguard-test-pg"
    21→DB_PORT=5433
    22→DB_USER="postgres"
    23→DB_PASS="testpass"
    24→DB_NAME="releaseguard_test"
    25→DATABASE_URL="postgres://${DB_USER}:${DB_PASS}@localhost:${DB_PORT}/${DB_NAME}?sslmode=disable"
    26→WEBHOOK_SECRET=[REDACTED]"
    27→SERVER_PORT=8089
    28→SERVER_PID=""
    29→BASE_URL="http://localhost:${SERVER_PORT}"
    30→
    31→RED='\033[0;31m'
    32→GREEN='\033[0;32m'
    33→YELLOW='\033[0;33m'
    34→BOLD='\033[1m'
    35→NC='\033[0m'
    36→
    37→TESTS_PASSED=0
    38→TESTS_FAILED=0
    39→TESTS_TOTAL=0
    40→
    41→# --- Helpers ---
    42→cleanup() {
    43→    echo ""
    44→    echo "--- Cleanup ---"
    45→    if [ -n "$SERVER_PID" ] && kill -0 "$SERVER_PID" 2>/dev/null; then
    46→        kill "$SERVER_PID" 2>/dev/null || true
    47→        wait "$SERVER_PID" 2>/dev/null || true
    48→        echo "Server stopped"
    49→    fi
    50→    docker rm -f "$CONTAINER_NAME" 2>/dev/null || true
    51→    echo "Postgres container removed"
    52→}
    53→trap cleanup EXIT
    54→
    55→fail() {
    56→    TESTS_FAILED=$((TESTS_FAILED + 1))
    57→    TESTS_TOTAL=$((TESTS_TOTAL + 1))
    58→    echo -e "  ${RED}FAIL: $1${NC}" >&2
    59→}
    60→
    61→pass() {
    62→    TESTS_PASSED=$((TESTS_PASSED + 1))
    63→    TESTS_TOTAL=$((TESTS_TOTAL + 1))
    64→    echo -e "  ${GREEN}PASS: $1${NC}"
    65→}
    66→
    67→section() {
    68→    echo ""
    69→    echo -e "${BOLD}--- $1 ---${NC}"
    70→}
    71→
    72→# Make an API call. Sets globals: HTTP_CODE, API_BODY.
    73→# Usage: api METHOD /path [-d 'json']
    74→api() {
    75→    local method="$1" path="$2"
    76→    shift 2
    77→    HTTP_CODE=$(curl -s -o /tmp/api-response.txt -w "%{http_code}" -X "$method" "${BASE_URL}${path}" \
    78→        -H "Content-Type: application/json" "$@")
    79→    API_BODY=$(cat /tmp/api-response.txt)
    80→}
    81→
    82→# Extract .data.id from API_BODY
    83→extract_id() {
    84→    echo "$API_BODY" | jq -r '.data.id'
    85→}
    86→
    87→# --- 1. Start Postgres ---
    88→section "Starting Postgres on port $DB_PORT"
    89→docker rm -f "$CONTAINER_NAME" 2>/dev/null || true
    90→docker run -d --name "$CONTAINER_NAME" \
    91→    -e POSTGRES_DB="$DB_NAME" \
    92→    -e POSTGRES_USER="$DB_USER" \
    93→    -e POSTGRES_PASSWORD="$DB_PASS" \
    94→    -p "${DB_PORT}:5432" \
    95→    postgres:16 >/dev/null
    96→
    97→echo "Waiting for Postgres..."
    98→for i in $(seq 1 30); do
    99→    if docker exec "$CONTAINER_NAME" pg_isready -U "$DB_USER" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
   100→        echo "Postgres ready"
   101→        break
   102→    fi
   103→    if [ "$i" -eq 30 ]; then
   104→        echo "Postgres did not become ready in 30s"
   105→        exit 1
   106→    fi
   107→    sleep 1
   108→done
   109→
   110→# --- 2. Build and start server ---
   111→section "Building server"
   112→cd "$PROJECT_ROOT"
   113→go build -o /tmp/releaseguard-test ./cmd/server/
   114→pass "Build succeeded"
   115→
   116→section "Starting server"
   117→DATABASE_URL="$DATABASE_URL" \
   118→GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET="$WEBHOOK_SECRET" \
   119→LISTEN_ADDR=":${SERVER_PORT}" \
   120→NO_AUTH=true \
   121→    /tmp/releaseguard-test >/tmp/releaseguard-test.log 2>&1 &
   122→SERVER_PID=$!
   123→sleep 2
   124→
   125→if ! kill -0 "$SERVER_PID" 2>/dev/null; then
   126→    echo "Server failed to start"
   127→    exit 1
   128→fi
   129→pass "Server started (PID $SERVER_PID)"
   130→
   131→# ============================================================
   132→# TEST SUITE
   133→# ============================================================
   134→
   135→# --- 3. Health check ---
   136→section "Health check"
   137→api GET "/api/v1/health"
   138→if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "200" ]; then
   139→    pass "Health endpoint returned 200"
   140→else
   141→    fail "Health endpoint returned $HTTP_CODE (expected 200)"
   142→fi
   143→
   144→# --- 4. Project CRUD ---
   145→section "Project CRUD"
   146→
   147→# Create project
   148→api POST "/api/v1/projects" -d '{"name":"integration-test-project","description":"Created by integration test"}'
   149→if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "201" ]; then
   150→    PROJECT_ID=$(extract_id)
   151→    pass "Create project (id: $PROJECT_ID)"
   152→else
   153→    fail "Create project returned $HTTP_CODE (expected 201). Body: $API_BODY"
   154→    PROJECT_ID=""
   155→fi
   156→
   157→# Get project
   158→if [ -n "$PROJECT_ID" ]; then
   159→    api GET "/api/v1/projects/$PROJECT_ID"
   160→    if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "200" ]; then
   161→        pass "Get project"
   162→    else
   163→        fail "Get project returned $HTTP_CODE (expected 200)"
   164→    fi
   165→fi
   166→
   167→# List projects
   168→api GET "/api/v1/projects"
   169→if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "200" ]; then
   170→    pass "List projects"
   171→else
   172→    fail "List projects returned $HTTP_CODE (expected 200)"
   173→fi
   174→
   175→# --- 5. Source CRUD ---
   176→section "Source CRUD"
   177→
   178→if [ -n "$PROJECT_ID" ]; then
   179→    # Create source (github provider matching the webhook repo)
   180→    api POST "/api/v1/projects/$PROJECT_ID/sources" \
   181→        -d '{"provider":"github","repository":"testorg/testrepo","poll_interval_seconds":3600,"enabled":true}'
   182→    if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "201" ]; then
   183→        SOURCE_ID=$(extract_id)
   184→        pass "Create source (id: $SOURCE_ID)"
   185→    else
   186→        fail "Create source returned $HTTP_CODE (expected 201). Body: $API_BODY"
   187→        SOURCE_ID=""
   188→    fi
   189→else
   190→    fail "Skipping source tests — no project"
   191→    SOURCE_ID=""
   192→fi
   193→
   194→# --- 6. GitHub webhook → release ingestion ---
   195→section "GitHub webhook flow"
   196→
   197→PAYLOAD='{"action":"published","release":{"tag_name":"v2.0.0","body":"## Release Notes\n* New feature","prerelease":false,"published_at":"2024-06-01T12:00:00Z"},"repository":{"full_name":"testorg/testrepo"}}'
   198→SIGNATURE=$(printf '%s' "$PAYLOAD" | openssl dgst -sha256 -hmac "$WEBHOOK_SECRET" | awk '{print $NF}')
   199→
   200→HTTP_CODE=$(curl -s -o /tmp/webhook-response.txt -w "%{http_code}" \
   201→    -X POST "${BASE_URL}/webhook/github" \
   202→    -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
   203→    -H "X-GitHub-Event: release" \
   204→    -H "X-Hub-Signature-256: sha256=${SIGNATURE}" \
   205→    -d "$PAYLOAD")
   206→
   207→if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "200" ]; then
   208→    pass "Webhook accepted (HTTP 200)"
   209→else
   210→    fail "Webhook returned HTTP $HTTP_CODE (expected 200). Body: $(cat /tmp/webhook-response.txt)"
   211→fi
   212→
   213→sleep 1
   214→
   215→# Verify release in database (using source_id + version, the actual schema)
   216→if [ -n "$SOURCE_ID" ]; then
   217→    ROW_COUNT=$(docker exec "$CONTAINER_NAME" psql -U "$DB_USER" -d "$DB_NAME" -tAc \
   218→        "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM releases WHERE source_id='$SOURCE_ID' AND version='v2.0.0';")
   219→    if [ "$ROW_COUNT" = "1" ]; then
   220→        pass "Release persisted in database (source_id=$SOURCE_ID, version=v2.0.0)"
   221→    else
   222→        fail "Expected 1 release row, got: '$ROW_COUNT'"
   223→    fi
   224→fi
   225→
   226→# Verify release via API
   227→if [ -n "$SOURCE_ID" ]; then
   228→    api GET "/api/v1/sources/$SOURCE_ID/releases"
   229→    if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "200" ]; then
   230→        RELEASE_COUNT=$(echo "$API_BODY" | jq '.data | length')
   231→        if [ "$RELEASE_COUNT" = "1" ]; then
   232→            pass "Release visible via API (1 release for source)"
   233→        else
   234→            fail "Expected 1 release via API, got: $RELEASE_COUNT"
   235→        fi
   236→    else
   237→        fail "List releases returned $HTTP_CODE (expected 200)"
   238→    fi
   239→fi
   240→
   241→# Verify River job was enqueued
   242→JOB_COUNT=$(docker exec "$CONTAINER_NAME" psql -U "$DB_USER" -d "$DB_NAME" -tAc \
   243→    "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM river_job WHERE kind='notify_release';")
   244→if [ "$JOB_COUNT" -ge 1 ]; then
   245→    pass "River notify_release job enqueued"
   246→else
   247→    fail "Expected at least 1 notify_release job, got: '$JOB_COUNT'"
   248→fi
   249→
   250→# --- 7. Duplicate webhook (idempotent skip) ---
   251→section "Duplicate webhook idempotency"
   252→
   253→HTTP_CODE=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" \
   254→    -X POST "${BASE_URL}/webhook/github" \
   255→    -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
   256→    -H "X-GitHub-Event: release" \
   257→    -H "X-Hub-Signature-256: sha256=${SIGNATURE}" \
   258→    -d "$PAYLOAD")
   259→
   260→if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "200" ]; then
   261→    pass "Duplicate webhook accepted (HTTP 200)"
   262→else
   263→    fail "Duplicate webhook returned HTTP $HTTP_CODE (expected 200)"
   264→fi
   265→
   266→sleep 1
   267→
   268→if [ -n "$SOURCE_ID" ]; then
   269→    ROW_COUNT=$(docker exec "$CONTAINER_NAME" psql -U "$DB_USER" -d "$DB_NAME" -tAc \
   270→        "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM releases WHERE source_id='$SOURCE_ID' AND version='v2.0.0';")
   271→    if [ "$ROW_COUNT" = "1" ]; then
   272→        pass "Duplicate correctly skipped (still 1 row)"
   273→    else
   274→        fail "Duplicate was not skipped — got $ROW_COUNT rows instead of 1"
   275→    fi
   276→fi
   277→
   278→# --- 8. Invalid signature rejection ---
   279→section "Invalid signature rejection"
   280→
   281→HTTP_CODE=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" \
   282→    -X POST "${BASE_URL}/webhook/github" \
   283→    -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
   284→    -H "X-GitHub-Event: release" \
   285→    -H "X-Hub-Signature-256: sha256=invalidsignature" \
   286→    -d "$PAYLOAD")
   287→
   288→if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "403" ]; then
   289→    pass "Invalid signature rejected (HTTP 403)"
   290→else
   291→    fail "Invalid signature returned HTTP $HTTP_CODE (expected 403)"
   292→fi
   293→
   294→# --- 9. Channel & subscription CRUD ---
   295→section "Channel & subscription CRUD"
   296→
   297→# Create channel
   298→api POST "/api/v1/channels" \
   299→    -d '{"name":"test-webhook","type":"webhook","config":{"url":"https://example.com/hook"}}'
   300→if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "201" ]; then
   301→    CHANNEL_ID=$(extract_id)
   302→    pass "Create channel (id: $CHANNEL_ID)"
   303→else
   304→    fail "Create channel returned $HTTP_CODE (expected 201). Body: $API_BODY"
   305→    CHANNEL_ID=""
   306→fi
   307→
   308→# Create source subscription
   309→if [ -n "$CHANNEL_ID" ] && [ -n "$SOURCE_ID" ]; then
   310→    api POST "/api/v1/subscriptions" \
   311→        -d "{\"type\":\"source\",\"channel_id\":\"$CHANNEL_ID\",\"source_id\":\"$SOURCE_ID\"}"
   312→    if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "201" ]; then
   313→        SUB_ID=$(extract_id)
   314→        pass "Create source subscription (id: $SUB_ID)"
   315→    else
   316→        fail "Create subscription returned $HTTP_CODE (expected 201). Body: $API_BODY"
   317→        SUB_ID=""
   318→    fi
   319→fi
   320→
   321→# List subscriptions
   322→api GET "/api/v1/subscriptions"
   323→if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "200" ]; then
   324→    pass "List subscriptions"
   325→else
   326→    fail "List subscriptions returned $HTTP_CODE (expected 200)"
   327→fi
   328→
   329→# Delete subscription
   330→if [ -n "${SUB_ID:-}" ]; then
   331→    api DELETE "/api/v1/subscriptions/$SUB_ID"
   332→    if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "204" ]; then
   333→        pass "Delete subscription"
   334→    else
   335→        fail "Delete subscription returned $HTTP_CODE (expected 204)"
   336→    fi
   337→fi
   338→
   339→# Delete channel
   340→if [ -n "${CHANNEL_ID:-}" ]; then
   341→    api DELETE "/api/v1/channels/$CHANNEL_ID"
   342→    if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "204" ]; then
   343→        pass "Delete channel"
   344→    else
   345→        fail "Delete channel returned $HTTP_CODE (expected 204)"
   346→    fi
   347→fi
   348→
   349→# --- 10. Cleanup test data ---
   350→section "Cleanup test data"
   351→
   352→if [ -n "$PROJECT_ID" ]; then
   353→    api DELETE "/api/v1/projects/$PROJECT_ID"
   354→    if [ "$HTTP_CODE" = "204" ]; then
   355→        pass "Delete project (cascades sources, releases)"
   356→    else
   357→        fail "Delete project returned $HTTP_CODE (expected 204)"
   358→    fi
   359→fi
   360→
   361→# ============================================================
   362→# SUMMARY
   363→# ============================================================
   364→echo ""
   365→echo -e "${BOLD}============================================================${NC}"
   366→echo -e "${BOLD}  Test Results: $TESTS_PASSED passed, $TESTS_FAILED failed (${TESTS_TOTAL} total)${NC}"

   371→    exit 1
   372→else
   373→    echo -e "${GREEN}  ALL TESTS PASSED${NC}"
   374→    exit 0
   375→fi

docs/plans/2026-02-25-pivot-design.md:5:Major pivot of ReleaseGuard from a pipeline-centric release processing system to an agent-driven release intelligence platform. Perfect! Now I have all the information needed. Let me compile a comprehensive summary for you.

---

## Project Context Summary: Changelogue

### 1. Root-Level File Structure

```
/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/
├── ARCH.md                    # System architecture & design
├── CLAUDE.md                  # Project guidelines for Claude Code
├── DESIGN.md                  # Detailed component & database design
├── Makefile                   # Build & development commands
├── docker-compose.yml         # PostgreSQL service definition
├── go.mod                      # Go module dependencies
├── cmd/server/                # Main Go application entry
├── internal/                  # Core backend packages
│   ├── agent/                 # ADK-Go agent orchestration
│   ├── api/                   # REST API & middleware
│   ├── ingestion/             # Polling workers & webhooks
│   ├── notification/          # Routing & channel implementations
│   ├── queue/                 # River job queue setup
│   ├── db/                    # Connection pool & migrations
│   └── models/                # Shared domain structs
├── web/                       # Next.js frontend app
├── deployments/               # Dockerfiles & deployment scripts
└── scripts/                   # Integration test scripts
```

### 2. What the Project Does

**Changelogue** is an **agent-driven release intelligence platform** that:

- **Polls upstream registries** (Docker Hub, GitHub) for new software releases
- **Sends source-level notifications** immediately to subscribed channels (Slack, Discord, PagerDuty, webhooks)
- **Uses LLM agents** (Google ADK-Go with Gemini) to produce semantic, AI-generated release reports
- **Centralizes release discovery** across multiple sources for a single piece of software ("project")
- **Provides a dashboard** (Next.js React UI) for managing projects, sources, subscriptions, and viewing releases

**Key Problem Solved:** Reduces "release noise" by providing intelligent filtering, correlation, and prioritization of updates across multiple upstream registries.

### 3. Tech Stack

| Component | Technology | Version |
|-----------|-----------|---------|
| **Backend** | Go | 1.25 |
| **Frontend** | Next.js + React + Tailwind | 16.1.6 / 19.2.3 |
| **Database/Queue/PubSub** | PostgreSQL + River | 16 / 0.31.0 |
| **Agent Intelligence** | Google ADK-Go | 0.5.0 |
| **LLM** | Google Gemini | via genai v1.40.0 |
| **Deployment** | Single binary (Go `//go:embed`) | — |

### 4. Build & Run Commands (from Makefile)

**Backend:**
```bash
make build              # go build -o changelogue ./cmd/server
make run                # Build + run with NO_AUTH=true
make test               # go test ./...
make vet                # go vet ./...
```

**Frontend:**
```bash
make frontend-install   # cd web && npm install
make frontend-dev       # cd web && npm run dev
make frontend-build     # cd web && npm run build
```

**Infrastructure:**
```bash
make up                 # docker compose up -d (Postgres)
make down               # docker compose down
make db-reset           # Reset Postgres database
make dev                # make up + make run (one command)
make integration-test   # bash scripts/integration-test.sh
make clean              # Remove binary + stop containers + remove volumes
```

### 5. Docker Compose Setup

**Single service:** PostgreSQL 16

```yaml
services:
  postgres:
    image: postgres:16
    container: changelogue-pg
    port: 5432:5432
    credentials: user=postgres, pass=postgres
    db: changelogue
    healthcheck: pg_isready check every 5s
```

### 6. Frontend Dependencies (web/package.json)

**Runtime:**
- `next@16.1.6` — React framework
- `react@19.2.3`, `react-dom@19.2.3` — UI library
- `tailwindcss@4` (via `@tailwindcss/postcss`) — Styling
- `radix-ui@1.4.3`, `shadcn@3.8.5` — Component libraries
- `lucide-react`, `react-icons` — Icon libraries
- `swr@2.4.0` — Data fetching (Next.js recommended)
- `class-variance-authority`, `clsx`, `tailwind-merge` — CSS utilities

**DevDependencies:**
- TypeScript, ESLint (Next.js config), Tailwind CSS, tw-animate-css

**Scripts:**
- `dev` — `next dev` (dev server)
- `build` — `next build` (static export for embedding)
- `start` — `next start`
- `lint` — `eslint`

### 7. Architecture Overview

**Four Decoupled Layers (PostgreSQL as the backbone):**

1. **Ingestion Layer** — Polling workers (Docker Hub, GitHub) → `IIngestionSource` interface → `releases` table
2. **Notification Routing** — River `notify_release` jobs → `Sender` interface → Slack/Discord/PagerDuty/webhooks
3. **Agent Layer** — River `agent_run` jobs → ADK-Go agent with tools → `semantic_releases` table
4. **Routing & Output** — Project-level subscriptions → Semantic release notifications

**Critical Pattern:** Transactional Outbox — Release insert + River job enqueue happen in one TX (zero-loss guarantee).

### 8. Database Schema (PostgreSQL)

**Core tables:**
- `projects` — Central entity (software you track)
- `sources` — Ingestion sources per project (Docker Hub repo, GitHub repo, etc.)
- `releases` — Source-level releases detected via polling
- `semantic_releases` — AI-generated project-level analysis reports
- `semantic_release_sources` — Join table linking semantic → source releases
- `context_sources` — Read-only references (runbooks, docs) for agent research
- `notification_channels` — Slack, Discord, PagerDuty, webhooks
- `subscriptions` — Source-level (raw releases) or project-level (semantic releases) routing
- `agent_runs` — Observability: who ran the agent, when, with what prompt, status
- `api_keys` — Bearer tokens for API auth

### 9. Data Flow Lifecycle

```
Polling Worker detects new version
    ↓
Single TX: INSERT release + enqueue River notify_release job
    ↓
NotifyWorker sends source-level notifications (immediate)
    ↓
IF agent rules match: enqueue River agent_run job
    ↓
AgentWorker runs ADK-Go agent:
  • Fetches recent releases via get_releases tool
  • Inspects details via get_release_detail tool
  • Consults context sources via list_context_sources tool
  • Produces SemanticRelease with structured SemanticReport
    ↓
PostgreSQL trigger fires NOTIFY (pg_notify)
    ↓
Dashboard clients receive SSE real-time updates
    ↓
Project-level subscribers notified with AI report
```

### 10. Key Design Features

- **Zero-Loss:** Transactional Outbox pattern ensures no events lost during restarts
- **Abstracted I/O:** `IIngestionSource` (providers), `Sender` (channels) — easy to add new registries/channels
- **Graceful Degradation:** If `GOOGLE_API_KEY` not set, agent worker doesn't register; rest of system works
- **Idempotency:** `UNIQUE(source_id, version)` on releases prevents duplicate notifications
- **Source-Level Filtering:** Exclusion rules applied before events enter system
- **Dead-Letter Queue:** Failed River jobs tracked for manual intervention
- **Single Binary:** Go `//go:embed` serves Next.js static export directly

### 11. Frontend Architecture (Next.js)

- **Framework:** Next.js 16 (App Router) + React 19
- **Styling:** Tailwind CSS v4 via PostCSS
- **Components:** Radix UI + shadcn for UI components
- **Data Fetching:** SWR (Stale-While-Revalidate)
- **Features:** Manage projects, sources, subscriptions, view releases, semantic reports, real-time updates via SSE

### 12. Existing README

Only `web/README.md` exists (generic Next.js bootstrap template). **No main project README at root level** — this is what you'll be writing.

---

## Key Takeaways for README

1. **Headline:** "AI-Powered Release Intelligence Platform" — polls registries, routes notifications, analyzes with LLMs
2. **Tech Story:** Go backend + Next.js frontend + PostgreSQL engine + Google Gemini agents
3. **Core Value:** Reduces release noise, provides ML-driven risk/urgency scoring, unifies dashboards
4. **Getting Started:** 1) `make dev` (Postgres + server), 2) `make frontend-dev` (Next.js on 3000), 3) Open http://localhost:8080
5. **Architecture:** Event-driven (Transactional Outbox), four-layer design, PostgreSQL as backbone
6. **Extensibility:** Add new registries (implement `IIngestionSource`), add new channels (implement `Sender`)
7. **Deployment:** Single binary with embedded frontend — `./changelogue`
8. **Status:** Production-ready with agent orchestration (phase-based), graceful degradation

All detailed architecture and design specs are in `ARCH.md` and `DESIGN.md` — the README should be an executive summary pointing to those for deep dives.

{

  "content": "# Changelogue\n\nAgent-driven release intelligence — polls upstream registries, routes notifications, and uses LLM agents to produce semantic release reports.\n\n## What it does\n\n- **Discovers releases** by polling Docker Hub and GitHub on configurable intervals\n- **Routes notifications** to Slack, Discord, and webhooks the moment a new version lands\n- **Generates AI reports** via Google Gemini agents that research changelogs, assess risk, and summarize what changed\n- **Serves a dashboard** (Next.js) for managing projects, sources, subscriptions, and browsing releases in real time\n\n## Architecture\n\n```\n┌─────────────┐     ┌──────────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐     ┌──────────────┐\n│  Ingestion   │────▸│   PostgreSQL +   │────▸│   Agent     │────▸│   Routing    │\n│  (polling)   │     │   River Queue    │     │  (ADK-Go)   │     │  (channels)  │\n└─────────────┘     └──────────────────┘     └─────────────┘     └──────────────┘\n  Docker Hub          Transactional            Gemini LLM           Slack\n  GitHub              Outbox pattern           research tools       Discord\n                      LISTEN/NOTIFY → SSE                           Webhooks\n```\n\nRelease insert and job enqueue happen in a single SQL transaction — zero-loss guarantee.\n\nSee [ARCH.md](ARCH.md) and [DESIGN.md](DESIGN.md) for the full design.\n\n## Quick start\n\n**Prerequisites:** Go 1.25+, Docker, Node.js 20+\n\n```bash\n# Start Postgres and the server (NO_AUTH mode)\nmake dev\n\n# In another terminal — start the frontend\nmake frontend-install\nmake frontend-dev\n```\n\nThe API runs on `localhost:8080`, the dashboard on `localhost:3000`.\n\n## Environment variables\n\n| Variable | Default | Purpose |\n|----------|---------|---------|\n| `DATABASE_URL` | `postgres://localhost:5432/changelogue?sslmode=disable` | PostgreSQL connection |\n| `LISTEN_ADDR` | `:8080` | HTTP server bind address |\n| `GOOGLE_API_KEY` | _(empty)_ | Gemini API key for agent worker (disabled if unset) |\n\n## Project structure\n\n```\ncmd/server/          Entry point — wires all layers together\ninternal/\n  agent/             ADK-Go agent orchestrator, tools, and worker\n  api/               REST API, SSE, middleware, auth\n  db/                Connection pool and migrations\n  ingestion/         Polling sources (Docker Hub, GitHub Atom)\n  models/            Shared domain types\n  queue/             River job definitions and client\n  routing/           Notification channels and delivery worker\nweb/                 Next.js dashboard (React + Tailwind + shadcn)\nscripts/             Integration test harness\n```\n\n## Extending\n\n**Add a registry provider** — implement `IIngestionSource` in `internal/ingestion/source.go`:\n\n```go\ntype IIngestionSource interface {\n    FetchNewReleases(ctx context.Context) ([]IngestionResult, error)\n}\n```\n\n**Add a notification channel** — implement `Sender` in `internal/routing/sender.go`:\n\n```go\ntype Sender interface {\n    Send(ctx context.Context, channel models.NotificationChannel, msg Message) error\n}\n```\n\n## Useful commands\n\n```bash\nmake build              # go build -o changelogue ./cmd/server\nmake test               # go test ./...\nmake vet                # go vet ./...\nmake integration-test   # full integration test (spins up its own Postgres)\nmake db-reset           # drop and recreate the local database\nmake clean              # remove binary, stop containers, delete volumes\n```\n"
}
```

    26→Release insert and job enqueue happen in a single SQL transaction — zero-loss guarantee.
    27→
    28→See [ARCH.md](ARCH.md) and [DESIGN.md](DESIGN.md) for the full design.
    29→
    30→## Quick start
    31→
    32→**Prerequisites:** Go 1.25+, Docker, Node.js 20+
    33→
    34→```bash
    35→# Start Postgres and the server (NO_AUTH mode)
    36→make dev
    37→
    38→# In another terminal — start the frontend
    39→make frontend-install
    40→make frontend-dev
    41→```
    42→
    43→The API runs on `localhost:8080`, the dashboard on `localhost:3000`.
    44→
    45→## Environment variables
    46→
    47→| Variable | Default | Purpose |
    48→|----------|---------|---------|
    49→| `DATABASE_URL` | `postgres://localhost:5432/changelogue?sslmode=disable` | PostgreSQL connection |
    50→| `LISTEN_ADDR` | `:8080` | HTTP server bind address |
    51→| `GOOGLE_API_KEY` | _(empty)_ | Gemini API key for agent worker (disabled if unset) |
    52→
    53→## Project structure
    54→
    55→```
    56→cmd/server/          Entry point — wires all layers together
    57→internal/
    58→  agent/             ADK-Go agent orchestrator, tools, and worker
    59→  api/               REST API, SSE, middleware, auth
    60→  db/                Connection pool and migrations
    61→  ingestion/         Polling sources (Docker Hub, GitHub Atom)
    62→  models/            Shared domain types
    63→  queue/             River job definitions and client
    64→  routing/           Notification channels and delivery worker
    65→web/                 Next.js dashboard (React + Tailwind + shadcn)
    66→scripts/             Integration test harness
    67→```
    68→
    69→## Extending
    70→
    71→More providers (npm, PyPI, Helm, etc.) and channels (PagerDuty, email, etc.) are planned. Adding one is a single-interface implementation:
    72→
    73→**Add a registry provider** — implement `IIngestionSource` in `internal/ingestion/source.go`:
    74→
    75→```go
    76→type IIngestionSource interface {
    77→    FetchNewReleases(ctx context.Context) ([]IngestionResult, error)
    78→}
    79→```
    80→
    81→**Add a notification channel** — implement `Sender` in `internal/routing/sender.go`:
    82→
    83→```go
    84→type Sender interface {
    85→    Send(ctx context.Context, channel models.NotificationChannel, msg Message) error
    86→}
    87→```
    88→
    89→## Useful commands
    90→
    91→```bash
    92→make build              # go build -o changelogue ./cmd/server
    93→make test               # go test ./...
    94→make vet                # go vet ./...
    95→make integration-test   # full integration test (spins up its own Postgres)
    96→make db-reset           # drop and recreate the local database
    97→make clean              # remove binary, stop containers, delete volumes
    98→```
    99→

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

```
     1→# Architecture: Changelogue
     2→
     3→## Overview
     4→
     5→Changelogue is an event-driven, hybrid-architecture system designed to centralize release discovery, automate validation, manage configurations, and distribute targeted notifications. It combines the high-concurrency performance of a Go-based polling engine with the reasoning capabilities of LLM-based SRE agents. By leveraging PostgreSQL for both persistent storage and message brokering, the system maintains high reliability and transactional consistency within a streamlined, single-binary deployment.
     6→
     7→## 1. Tech Stack & Infrastructure
     8→
     9→* **Backend / Engine:** Go (Golang). Chosen for its lightweight concurrency model (Goroutines) to handle simultaneous polling across multiple registries.
    10→* **Frontend / Dashboard:** Next.js (React) with Tailwind CSS. Provides a fast, modern control center for viewing release streams and managing configurations.
    11→* **Database, Queue, & Event Bus:** PostgreSQL.
    12→* *Persistent Storage:* Standard relational tables for release metadata and system configurations.
    13→* *Task Queue:* Utilizes `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (via the **River** Go library) for robust, concurrent background job processing without race conditions.
    14→* *Pub/Sub:* Utilizes native `LISTEN` and `NOTIFY` for real-time broadcasting to connected clients.
    15→
    16→
    17→* **Intelligence Layer:** LLMs (Gemini / GPT-4o-mini) orchestrated via agent frameworks (e.g., LangGraph or Claude Agent SDK) for semantic changelog analysis and autonomous validation.
    18→* **Packaging:** Single binary deployment utilizing Go's `//go:embed` to serve the Next.js static export directly from the Go server.
    19→
    20→## 2. System Design & Abstractions
    21→
    22→The system is decoupled into four primary layers communicating entirely through PostgreSQL using the Transactional Outbox pattern:
    23→
    24→1. **Ingestion Layer** — Polling workers and webhook handlers discover new releases from upstream registries.
    25→2. **Notification Routing** — River workers send immediate notifications to source-level subscribers on new releases.
    26→3. **Agent Layer** — ADK-Go agents research releases, consult context sources, and produce semantic release reports.
    27→4. **Routing & Output** — Notification channels (Slack, PagerDuty, webhooks) deliver alerts via `INotificationChannel`.
    28→
    29→A **Project** is the central domain entity — it groups multiple ingestion sources, context sources, and notification subscriptions under a single tracked piece of software.
    30→
    31→```mermaid
    32→graph LR
    33→    %% Data Flow
    34→    subgraph Dashboard [API & Dashboard]
    35→        API[REST API] --> |CRUD| PG
    36→        API --> |SSE| Browser[Next.js Dashboard]
    37→    end
    38→
    39→    subgraph Ingestion [Ingestion Layer]
    40→        I_Hub(Docker Hub Poller) --> |IIngestionSource| Engine
    41→        I_Git(GitHub Webhooks) --> |HTTP Handler| Engine
    42→    end
    43→
    44→    subgraph Core [Event Bus & Queue - PostgreSQL]
    45→        Engine((Go Engine)) --> |INSERT release + jobs\nTransactional Outbox| PG[(PostgreSQL)]
    46→    end
    47→
    48→    subgraph Notification [Notification Routing]
    49→        PG --> |SKIP LOCKED\nRiver notify_release| NotifyWorker[Notify Worker]
    50→        NotifyWorker --> |INotificationChannel| O_Slack(Slack/Teams)
    51→        NotifyWorker --> |INotificationChannel| O_PD(PagerDuty)
    52→        NotifyWorker --> |INotificationChannel| O_Hook(Custom Webhooks)
    53→    end
    54→
    55→    subgraph AgentLayer [Agent Layer]
    56→        PG --> |SKIP LOCKED\nRiver agent_run| AgentWorker[Agent Worker]
    57→        AgentWorker --> |ADK-Go| LLM[LLM Provider]
    58→        AgentWorker --> |Research| ContextSources[Context Sources]
    59→        AgentWorker --> |Produce| SemanticRelease[Semantic Release + Report]
    60→    end
    61→
    62→```
    63→
    64→### Entity Relationship Model
    65→
    66→```mermaid
    67→erDiagram
    68→    projects ||--o{ sources : "has many"
    69→    projects ||--o{ context_sources : "has many"
    70→    projects ||--o{ semantic_releases : "has many"
    71→    projects ||--o{ agent_runs : "has many"
    72→    sources ||--o{ releases : "produces"
    73→    subscriptions }o--|| notification_channels : "routes to"
    74→    subscriptions }o--o| sources : "source-level"
    75→    subscriptions }o--o| projects : "project-level"
    76→    semantic_releases ||--o{ semantic_release_sources : "composed of"
    77→    releases ||--o{ semantic_release_sources : "contributes to"
    78→
    79→    projects {
    80→        uuid id PK
    81→        string name UK
    82→        text agent_prompt
    83→        jsonb agent_rules
    84→    }
    85→    sources {
    86→        uuid id PK
    87→        uuid project_id FK
    88→        string provider
    89→        string repository UK
    90→        boolean enabled
    91→    }
    92→    releases {
    93→        uuid id PK
    94→        uuid source_id FK
    95→        string version
    96→        jsonb raw_data
    97→    }
    98→    semantic_releases {
    99→        uuid id PK
   100→        uuid project_id FK
   101→        string version
   102→        jsonb report
   103→        string status
   104→    }
   105→    agent_runs {
   106→        uuid id PK
   107→        uuid project_id FK
   108→        uuid semantic_release_id FK
   109→        string trigger
   110→        string status
   111→    }
   112→    subscriptions {
   113→        uuid id PK
   114→        uuid channel_id FK
   115→        string type
   116→        uuid source_id FK
   117→        uuid project_id FK
   118→    }
   119→    notification_channels {
   120→        uuid id PK
   121→        string type
   122→        string name
   123→        jsonb config
   124→    }
   125→```
   126→
   127→### 2.1 The Event-Driven Backbone (PostgreSQL Only)
   128→
   129→Components do not call each other synchronously. Instead, they rely on PostgreSQL to guarantee delivery:
   130→
   131→* **The Transactional Outbox:** When a new release is detected, the ingestion worker writes the metadata to the `releases` table (linked to its `source_id`) and simultaneously enqueues River jobs (notification and/or agent) *within the exact same SQL transaction*. This guarantees no events are ever lost.
   132→* **Real-time Pub/Sub:** Database triggers use `pg_notify` to broadcast lightweight events (e.g., telling the Next.js UI via SSE to refresh) over standard Postgres connections using `LISTEN`.
   133→* **Reliable Queues:** River workers process jobs using `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, ensuring exactly-once processing for both notification delivery and agent runs.
   134→* **Project-Centric Model:** All data flows through the `projects` → `sources` → `releases` hierarchy. Subscriptions can attach at the source level (for raw release notifications) or the project level (for semantic release notifications).
   135→
   136→### 2.2 Provider Interfaces (I/O)
   137→
   138→All external integrations are abstracted behind strict Go interfaces.
   139→
   140→* `IIngestionSource`: Standardizes how polling workers fetch data. Adding a new registry (like npm or NuGet) only requires implementing this interface. Each implementation maps to a `source_type` in the database.
   141→* `INotificationChannel`: Standardizes output routing. Each implementation maps to a `type` in the `notification_channels` table (Slack, PagerDuty, custom webhooks).
   142→
   143→### 2.3 REST API & Dashboard
   144→
   145→The Go server exposes a RESTful API (`/api/v1`) serving the Next.js dashboard and external consumers:
   146→
   147→* **Resource CRUD:** Projects, sources, subscriptions, notification channels — all manageable through the API.
   148→* **Read-Only Releases:** Releases and semantic releases are queryable but not writable via the API — they're created exclusively through the ingestion layer and agent runs.
   149→* **SSE Real-Time Events:** `GET /api/v1/events` streams server-sent events backed by PostgreSQL `LISTEN/NOTIFY`, pushing release and semantic release updates to connected dashboard clients.
   150→* **API Key Auth:** Bearer token authentication with hashed key storage. Webhooks use their own HMAC-based auth.
   151→* **Rate Limiting:** Per-key token bucket with standard `X-Ratelimit-*` response headers.
   152→
   153→### 2.4 Notification Routing
   154→
   155→When a new source release is detected, a `notify_release` River job is enqueued in the same transaction as the release insert. The notification worker:
   156→
   157→1. Resolves all source-level subscriptions for the release's source.
   158→2. For each subscription, sends a notification to the linked channel via `INotificationChannel`.
   159→
   160→This provides immediate, low-latency alerts for raw releases without waiting for AI analysis. Source-level subscriptions are simple: "tell me when this source has a new release."
   161→
   162→### 2.5 Agent Layer (Implemented)
   163→
   164→For project-level intelligence, an `agent_run` River job triggers an LLM agent (via ADK-Go v0.5.0) that:
   165→
   166→1. Gathers recent source releases for the project using the `get_releases` tool.
   167→2. Inspects individual release details (changelogs, raw data) using `get_release_detail`.
   168→3. Consults context sources (runbooks, docs, monitoring dashboards) using `list_context_sources`.
   169→4. Produces a `SemanticRelease` with a structured `SemanticReport` (summary, availability, adoption, urgency, recommendation).
   170→
   171→Agent behavior is configured per-project via `agent_prompt` (custom instructions) and `agent_rules` (structured triggers like `on_major_release`, `on_security_patch`, `version_pattern`). Agent runs are tracked in the `agent_runs` table for observability and auditability.
   172→
   173→**Implementation details (`internal/agent/`):**
   174→
   175→* **`tools.go`** — Three ADK function tools (`get_releases`, `get_release_detail`, `list_context_sources`) created via `functiontool.New()`. Tools are scoped to a project via `toolFactory` which holds the `AgentDataStore` and project ID.
   176→* **`orchestrator.go`** — `Orchestrator` manages the full agent lifecycle: loading project config, creating an ADK `llmagent` with Gemini as the LLM backend, running the agent via `runner.Runner`, parsing the JSON report, and persisting the `SemanticRelease` in a transaction.
   177→* **`worker.go`** — `AgentWorker` implements `river.Worker[queue.AgentJobArgs]`, loading the agent run from the store and delegating to `Orchestrator.RunAgent()`.
   178→* **Graceful degradation** — If `GOOGLE_API_KEY` is not set, the orchestrator is not created, the worker is not registered, and a warning is logged. The rest of the system continues to function. Agent jobs remain in the River queue until the key is configured.
   179→
   180→### 2.6 Agentic Tooling (Planned: SRE Validation)
   181→
   182→For deep validation, Changelogue will utilize SRE agents with a suite of abstracted tools:
   183→
   184→* `UpgradeBaseABoxConfig(version)`
   185→* `CheckAgentStatus(environment)`
   186→* `SyncOpsOpsack(payload)`
   187→This will allow the agent to autonomously deploy a sandbox, verify that the deployment is healthy, and rollback or alert on failure.
   188→
   189→## 3. Data Flow: Lifecycle of a Release Event
   190→
   191→1. **Discovery:** A Go worker polling Docker Hub detects a new base image tag for a configured source (e.g., the "Docker Hub" source under the "Go Runtime" project). Source-level exclusion filters (`exclude_version_regexp`, `exclude_prereleases`) are applied -- filtered versions are discarded immediately.
   192→2. **Ingestion & Transaction:** The worker stores the raw upstream payload and executes a single database transaction to insert the record into `releases` (linked to its `source_id`) and enqueue River jobs (`notify_release` for immediate notifications, optionally `agent_run` if the project's agent rules match).
   193→3. **Notification (Source-Level):** A River worker picks up the `notify_release` job, resolves source-level subscriptions, and sends alerts to configured notification channels (Slack, PagerDuty, webhooks).
   194→4. **Agent Analysis (Project-Level):** If triggered, a River worker picks up the `agent_run` job. The ADK-Go agent gathers recent releases, consults context sources, and produces a `SemanticRelease` with a structured report (summary, availability, adoption, urgency, recommendation).
   195→5. **Broadcast:** PostgreSQL triggers fire `NOTIFY` payloads on release insert and semantic release completion, pushing SSE events to connected dashboard clients.
   196→6. **Project-Level Notification:** When a semantic release is completed, project-level subscribers are notified with the AI-generated report (e.g., a critical PagerDuty alert for high-urgency releases, or a batched daily digest to Slack for low-priority updates).
   197→
   198→## 4. Directory Structure
   199→
   200→```text
   201→/changelogue
   202→├── cmd/
   203→│   └── server/          # Main Go application entry point
   204→├── internal/
   205→│   ├── api/             # REST API handlers, middleware, SSE broadcaster
   206→│   ├── ingestion/       # Polling workers and webhook handlers (IIngestionSource)
   207→│   ├── agent/           # ADK-Go agent for semantic release analysis
   208→│   ├── notification/    # Notification routing worker and channel implementations
   209→│   ├── queue/           # PostgreSQL River queue setup and job definitions
   210→│   ├── db/              # Connection pool and schema migrations
   211→│   └── models/          # Shared domain structs (Release, Project, SemanticRelease, etc.)
   212→├── web/                 # Next.js frontend application
   213→│   ├── app/
   214→│   └── components/
   215→├── deployments/         # Dockerfiles and Base A Box integration scripts
   216→└── go.mod
   217→
   218→```

> AGENT

Good catch — there are misalignments. Here's what the SVG shows vs the actual architecture:

**SVG currently shows:**
```
Upstream Registries → [Polls] → LLM Agents → [Produces] → Semantic Reports
                                            → [Routes]   → Notifications
```

**Actual architecture (from ARCH.md):**
```
Ingestion (polling) → PostgreSQL + River Queue → Notify Worker → Notifications (immediate, source-level)
                                               → Agent Worker  → Semantic Reports (then project-level notifications)
```

**Key misalignments:**

1. **Missing PostgreSQL** — the central hub (database + River queue) is missing entirely. The SVG skips straight from registries to LLM agents.
2. **Flow is wrong** — notifications happen *before* agents, not after. The Notify Worker sends immediate source-level alerts. The agent is a separate, parallel path.
3. **LLM Agents don't "Route"** — agents produce semantic reports. Notification routing is a separate worker.

The SVG needs to show PostgreSQL as the central node with two output paths: one to notifications (immediate) and one to the agent (which then produces semantic reports). Want me to redraw it to match the actual architecture?

> DEVELOPER

Sure

Here's the design:

**Logo: Changelog book with diff marker**

An open book/page icon with a subtle "change" indicator — a small diff-style line or delta mark on the page. Rendered in the brand orange (`#e8601a`) with the gradient to amber (`#f59e0b`). Clean geometric lines so it scales down to 16x16 favicon without losing detail.

**Where it goes:**
- **Sidebar** — replaces the orange dot, sits left of "Changelogue" text
- **Favicon** — `web/public/favicon.svg` (and referenced in layout.tsx)
- **Banner SVG** — optionally replaces the green dot next to the title

**What changes in the website:**
- `web/components/layout/sidebar.tsx` — swap the `<span>` dot for an `<img>` or inline SVG of the logo
- `web/app/layout.tsx` — add favicon `<link>` in metadata
- `web/public/favicon.svg` — new file
- `web/public/logo.svg` — new file (full-size version)
